<p class=Titlepageinfo>Abstract:</p>

<p class=Abstract>This document indicates the policy assertions for use with [<a
href="#wspolicy">WS-Policy</a>] which apply to WSS: SOAP Message Security [<a
href="#wss10">WSS10</a>, <a href="#wss11">WSS11</a>], [<a href="#wstrust">WS-Trust</a>]
and [<a href="#wssecureconversation">WS-SecureConversation</a>]. This document
incorporates Approved Errata approved by the Technical Committee on 25 April
2012.</p>

<div class=WordSection2>

<div style='border:none;border-top:solid gray 1.0pt;padding:6.0pt 0in 0in 0in'>

<h1><a name="_Toc325572692"></a><a name="_Toc182291471"></a><a
name="_Toc161826495">1<span style='font:7.0pt "Times New Roman"'>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;
</span>Introduction</a></h1>

</div>

<p class=Text><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>WS-Policy defines
a framework for allowing web services to express their constraints and
requirements. Such constraints and requirements are expressed as policy assertions.
This document defines a set of security policy assertions for use with the [<a
href="#wspolicy">WS-Policy</a>] framework with respect to security features
provided in WSS: SOAP Message Security [<a href="#wss10">WSS10</a>, <a
href="#wss11">WSS11</a>], [<a href="#wstrust">WS-Trust</a>] and [<a
href="#wssecureconversation">WS-SecureConversation</a>]. </span><span
style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>The assertions defined within this
specification have been designed to work independently of a specific version of
WS-Policy. At the time of the publication of this specification the versions of
WS-Policy known to correctly compose with this specification are WS-Policy 1.2
and 1.5. Within this specification the use of the namespace prefix wsp refers
generically to the WS-Policy namespace, not a specific version.</span><span
style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'> This document takes the approach of
defining a base set of assertions that describe how messages are to be secured.
Flexibility with respect to token types, cryptographic algorithms and
mechanisms used, including using transport level security is part of the design
and allows for evolution over time. The intent is to provide enough information
for compatibility and interoperability to be determined by web service
participants along with all information necessary to actually enable a
participant to engage in a secure exchange of messages.</span></p>

<p class=Text><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'><br>
Sections 11, 12 and all examples and all Appendices are non-normative. </span></p>

<h2><a name="_Toc325572693"></a><a name="_Toc182291472"></a><a
name="_Toc161826496"></a><a name="_Toc104103910">1.1 Example</a></h2>

<p class=Text><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>Table 1 shows an </span><span
style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>&quot;</span><span style='font-family:
"Arial","sans-serif"'>Effective Policy</span><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>&quot;</span><span
style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'> example, including binding assertions
and associated property assertions, token assertions and integrity and
confidentiality assertions. This example has a scope of [Endpoint Policy
Subject], but for brevity the attachment mechanism is not shown.</span></p>

<p class=MsoCaption><a name="_Ref102231935">Table </a>1: Example security
policy.</p>

<p class=CodeNumbered>(01)<span style='font:7.0pt "Times New Roman"'> </span>&lt;wsp:Policy
xmlns:wsp=&quot;...&quot; xmlns:sp=&quot;...&quot;&gt;</p>

<p class=CodeNumbered>(02)<span style='font:7.0pt "Times New Roman"'> </span>  &lt;sp:SymmetricBinding&gt;</p>

<p class=CodeNumbered>(03)<span style='font:7.0pt "Times New Roman"'> </span>    &lt;wsp:Policy&gt;</p>

<p class=CodeNumbered>(04)<span style='font:7.0pt "Times New Roman"'> </span>      &lt;sp:ProtectionToken&gt;</p>

<p class=CodeNumbered>(05)<span style='font:7.0pt "Times New Roman"'> </span>        &lt;wsp:Policy&gt;</p>

<p class=CodeNumbered>(06)<span style='font:7.0pt "Times New Roman"'> </span>          &lt;sp:Kerberos
sp:IncludeToken=&quot;.../IncludeToken/Once&quot; /&gt;</p>

<p class=CodeNumbered>(07)<span style='font:7.0pt "Times New Roman"'> </span>            &lt;wsp:Policy&gt;</p>

<p class=CodeNumbered>(08)<span style='font:7.0pt "Times New Roman"'> </span>              &lt;sp:WSSKerberosV5ApReqToken11/&gt;</p>

<p class=CodeNumbered>(09)<span style='font:7.0pt "Times New Roman"'> </span>            &lt;wsp:Policy&gt;</p>

<p class=CodeNumbered>(10)<span style='font:7.0pt "Times New Roman"'> </span>          &lt;/sp:Kerberos&gt;</p>

<p class=CodeNumbered>(11)<span style='font:7.0pt "Times New Roman"'> </span>        &lt;/wsp:Policy&gt;</p>

<p class=CodeNumbered>(12)<span style='font:7.0pt "Times New Roman"'> </span>      &lt;/sp:ProtectionToken&gt;</p>

<p class=CodeNumbered>(13)<span style='font:7.0pt "Times New Roman"'> </span>      &lt;sp:SignBeforeEncrypting
/&gt;</p>

<p class=CodeNumbered>(14)<span style='font:7.0pt "Times New Roman"'> </span>      &lt;sp:EncryptSignature
/&gt;</p>

<p class=CodeNumbered>(15)<span style='font:7.0pt "Times New Roman"'> </span>    &lt;/wsp:Policy&gt;</p>

<p class=CodeNumbered>(16)<span style='font:7.0pt "Times New Roman"'> </span>  &lt;/sp:SymmetricBinding&gt;</p>

<p class=CodeNumbered>(17)<span style='font:7.0pt "Times New Roman"'> </span>  &lt;sp:SignedParts&gt;</p>

<p class=CodeNumbered>(18)<span style='font:7.0pt "Times New Roman"'> </span>    &lt;sp:Body/&gt;</p>

<p class=CodeNumbered>(19)<span style='font:7.0pt "Times New Roman"'> </span>    &lt;sp:Header<br>
       Namespace=&quot;http://schemas.xmlsoap.org/ws/2004/08/addressing&quot;<br>
    /&gt;</p>

<p class=CodeNumbered>(20)<span style='font:7.0pt "Times New Roman"'> </span>  &lt;/sp:SignedParts&gt;</p>

<p class=CodeNumbered>(21)<span style='font:7.0pt "Times New Roman"'> </span>  &lt;sp:EncryptedParts&gt;</p>

<p class=CodeNumbered>(22)<span style='font:7.0pt "Times New Roman"'> </span>    &lt;sp:Body/&gt;</p>

<p class=CodeNumbered>(23)<span style='font:7.0pt "Times New Roman"'> </span>  &lt;/sp:EncryptedParts&gt;</p>

<p class=CodeNumbered>(24)<span style='font:7.0pt "Times New Roman"'> </span>&lt;/wsp:Policy&gt;</p>

<p class=Text><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>&nbsp;</span></p>

<p class=Text><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>Line 1 in Table 1 indicates
that this is a policy statement and that all assertions contained by the
wsp:Policy element are required to be satisfied. Line 2 indicates the kind of
security binding in force. Line 3 indicates a nested </span><span
style='font-family:"Courier New"'>wsp:Policy</span><span style='font-family:
"Arial","sans-serif"'> element which contains assertions that qualify the
behavior of the SymmetricBinding assertion. Line 4 indicates a ProtectionToken
assertion. Line 5 indicates a nested </span><span style='font-family:"Courier New"'>wsp:Policy</span><span
style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'> element which contains assertions
indicating the type of token to be used for the ProtectionToken. Lines 6 to 10
indicate that a Kerberos V5 APREQ token is to be used by both parties in a message
exchange for protection. Line 13 indicates that signatures are generated over
plaintext rather than ciphertext. Line 14 indicates that the signature over the
signed messages parts is required to be encrypted. Lines 17-20 indicate which
message parts are to be covered by the primary signature; in this case the
soap:Body element, indicated by Line 18 and any SOAP headers in the
WS-Addressing namespace, indicated by line 19. Lines 21-23 indicate which
message parts are to be encrypted; in this case just the soap:Body element,
indicated by Line 22</span><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>.</span></p>

<h2><a name="_Toc104103913">1.2  </a><a name="_Toc325572694"></a><a
name="_Toc182291473"></a><a name="_Toc161826497">Namespaces</a></h2>

<p class=Text><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>The XML namespace
URI that MUST be used by implementations of this specification is: </span></p>

<div style='border-top:solid windowtext 1.0pt;border-left:none;border-bottom:
solid windowtext 1.0pt;border-right:none;padding:3.0pt 0in 3.0pt 0in;
background:#D9D9D9;margin-left:.3in;margin-right:.3in'>

<p class=Code style='margin:0in;margin-bottom:.0001pt;background:#D9D9D9'>http://docs.oasis-open.org/ws-sx/ws-securitypolicy/200702</p>

</div>

<p class=MsoNormal>&nbsp;</p>

<p class=MsoNormal>Table 2 lists XML namespaces that are used in this specification.
The choice of any namespace prefix is arbitrary and not semantically
significant.</p>

<p class=MsoCaption><a name="_Ref99536081">Table </a>2: Prefixes and XML
Namespaces used in this specification.</p>

<table class=MsoNormalTable border=1 cellspacing=0 cellpadding=0
 style='border-collapse:collapse;border:none'>
 <tr>
  <td valign=top style='border:solid windowtext 1.0pt;padding:0in 5.4pt 0in 5.4pt'>
  <p class=text0><b><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>Prefix</span></b></p>
  </td>
  <td width=393 valign=top style='width:294.8pt;border:solid windowtext 1.0pt;
  border-left:none;padding:0in 5.4pt 0in 5.4pt'>
  <p class=text0><b><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>Namespace</span></b></p>
  </td>
  <td width=181 valign=top style='width:135.9pt;border:solid windowtext 1.0pt;
  border-left:none;padding:0in 5.4pt 0in 5.4pt'>
  <p class=text0><b><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>Specification(s)</span></b></p>
  </td>
 </tr>
 <tr>
  <td valign=top style='border:solid windowtext 1.0pt;border-top:none;
  padding:0in 5.4pt 0in 5.4pt'>
  <p class=text0><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>S</span></p>
  </td>
  <td width=393 valign=top style='width:294.8pt;border-top:none;border-left:
  none;border-bottom:solid windowtext 1.0pt;border-right:solid windowtext 1.0pt;
  padding:0in 5.4pt 0in 5.4pt'>
  <p class=text0><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>http://schemas.xmlsoap.org/soap/envelope/</span></p>
  </td>

  <p class=text0><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>[<a href="#soap">SOAP</a>]</span></p>
  </td>
 </tr>
 <tr>
  <td valign=top style='border:solid windowtext 1.0pt;border-top:none;
  padding:0in 5.4pt 0in 5.4pt'>

be retrieved from the following address:</p>

background:#D9D9D9'>

<h2><a name="_Toc325572696"></a><a name="_Toc182291475"></a><a
name="_Toc161826499">1.4 Terminology</a></h2>

<p class=DefinedTerm><span class=LabelEmbedded><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>Policy</span></span><span
style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'> - <span class=TextChar1><span
style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>A collection of policy alternatives</span></span>.</span></p>

<p class=DefinedTerm><b><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>Policy
Alternative</span></b><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'> - A
collection of policy assertions.</span></p>

<p class=DefinedTerm><b><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>Policy
Assertion</span></b><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'> - An
individual requirement, capability, other property, or a behavior.</span></p>

<p class=DefinedTerm><b><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>Initiator</span></b><span
style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'> - The role sending the initial
message in a message exchange.</span></p>

<p class=DefinedTerm><b><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>Recipient</span></b><span
style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'> - The targeted role to process the
initial message in a message exchange.</span></p>

<p class=DefinedTerm><b><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>Security
Binding</span></b><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'> - A set of
properties that together provide enough information to secure a given message
exchange.</span></p>

<p class=DefinedTerm><b><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>Security
Binding Property</span></b><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'> - A
particular aspect of securing an exchange of messages.</span></p>

<p class=DefinedTerm><b><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>Security
Binding Assertion</span></b><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'> - A
policy assertion that identifies the type of security binding being used to
secure an exchange of messages.</span></p>

<p class=DefinedTerm><b><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>Security
Binding Property Assertion</span></b><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>
- <span class=TextChar1><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>A policy
assertion that specifies a particular value for a particular aspect of securing
an exchange of message</span></span>.</span></p>

<p class=DefinedTerm><b><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>Assertion
Parameter</span></b><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'> - An
element of variability within a policy assertion.</span></p>

<p class=DefinedTerm><b><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>Token
Assertion</span></b><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'> -Describes
a token requirement. Token assertions defined within a security binding are
used to satisfy protection requirements.</span></p>

<p class=DefinedTerm><b><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>Supporting
Token</span></b><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'> - A token used
to provide additional claims.</span></p>

<h3><a name="_Toc325572697"></a><a name="_Toc182291476"></a><a
name="_Toc161826500">1.4.1 Notational Conventions</a></h3>

<p class=MsoNormal>The key words “MUST”, “MUST NOT”, “REQUIRED”, “SHALL”,
“SHALL NOT”, “SHOULD”, “SHOULD NOT”, “RECOMMENDED”, “MAY”, and “OPTIONAL” in
this document are to be interpreted as described in [<a href="#rfc2119">RFC2119</a>].</p>

<p class=MsoNormal>This specification uses the following syntax to define
outlines for assertions: </p>

<ul style='margin-top:0in' type=disc>
 <li class=MsoNormal>The syntax appears as an XML instance, but values in
     italics indicate data types instead of literal values.</li>
 <li class=MsoNormal>Characters are appended to elements and attributes to
     indicate cardinality:</li>
 <ul style='margin-top:0in' type=circle>
  <li class=MsoNormal>&quot;?&quot; (0 or 1)</li>
  <li class=MsoNormal>&quot;*&quot; (0 or more)</li>
  <li class=MsoNormal>&quot;+&quot; (1 or more)</li>
 </ul>
 <li class=MsoNormal>The character &quot;|&quot; is used to indicate a choice
     between alternatives.</li>
 <li class=MsoNormal>The characters &quot;(&quot; and &quot;)&quot; are used to
     indicate that contained items are to be treated as a group with respect to
     cardinality or choice.</li>
 <li class=MsoNormal>The characters &quot;[&quot; and &quot;]&quot; are used to
     call out references and property names.</li>
 <li class=MsoNormal>Ellipses (i.e., &quot;...&quot;) indicate points of
     extensibility. Additional children and/or attributes MAY be added at the
     indicated extension points but MUST NOT contradict the semantics of the
     parent and/or owner, respectively. By default, if a receiver does not
     recognize an extension, the receiver SHOULD ignore the extension;
     exceptions to this processing rule, if any, are clearly indicated below.</li>
 <li class=MsoNormal>XML namespace prefixes (see Table 2) are used to indicate
     the namespace of the element being defined.</li>
</ul>

<p class=MsoNormal>&nbsp;</p>

<p class=MsoNormal><span style='color:black'>Elements and Attributes defined by
this specification are referred to in the text of this document using XPath 1.0
expressions. Extensibility points are referred to using an extended version of
this syntax:</span></p>

<ul style='margin-top:0in' type=disc>
 <li class=MsoNormal style='color:black'>An element extensibility point is
     referred to using {any} in place of the element name. This indicates that
     any element name can be used, from any namespace other than the namespace
     of this specification.</li>
 <li class=MsoNormal style='color:black'>An attribute extensibility point is
     referred to using @{any} in place of the attribute name. This indicates
     that any attribute name can be used, from any namespace other than the
     namespace of this specification.</li>
</ul>

<p class=MsoNormal>Extensibility points in the exemplar MAY NOT be described in
the corresponding text.</p>

<p class=Text><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>In this document
reference is made to the </span><span class=CodeChar><span style='font-size:
9.0pt'>wsu:Id</span></span><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'> attribute
and the </span><span class=CodeEmbedded>wsu:Created</span><span
style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'> and </span><span class=CodeEmbedded>wsu:Expires</span><span
style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'> elements in a utility schema (</span><span
style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'><a href="https://docs.oasis-open.org/wss/2004/01/oasis-200401-wss-wssecurity-utility-1.0.xsd">http://docs.oasis-open.org/wss/2004/01/oasis-200401-wss-wssecurity-utility-1.0.xsd</a></span><span
style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>). The </span><span class=CodeChar><span
style='font-size:9.0pt'>wsu:Id</span></span><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>
attribute and the </span><span class=CodeEmbedded>wsu:Created </span><span
style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>and </span><span class=CodeEmbedded>wsu:Expires</span><span
style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'> elements were added to the utility
schema with the intent that other specifications requiring such an ID type
attribute or timestamp element could reference it (as is done here).</span></p>

<p class=Text><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>&nbsp;</span></p>

<p class=Text><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>WS-SecurityPolicy is
designed to work with the general Web Services framework including WSDL service
descriptions, UDDI businessServices and bindingTemplates and <a href="#soap">SOAP</a>
message structure and message processing model, and WS-SecurityPolicy SHOULD be
applicable to any version of <a href="#soap">SOAP</a>. The current SOAP </span><span
style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>1.2</span><span style='font-family:
"Arial","sans-serif"'> namespace URI is used herein to provide detailed
examples, but there is no intention to limit the applicability of this
specification to a single version of <a href="#soap">SOAP</a>.</span></p>

<h2><a name="_Toc325572698"></a><a name="_Toc182291477"></a><a
name="_Toc161826501"></a><a name="_Toc12011611"></a><a name="_Ref7502892">1.5 Normative</a>
References</h2>

<p class=MsoNormal style='margin-top:4.0pt;margin-right:0in;margin-bottom:0in;
margin-left:153.0pt;margin-bottom:.0001pt;text-indent:-153.0pt'><a
name=rfc2119></a>[RFC2119]                                    S. Bradner,
&quot;Key words for use in RFCs to Indicate Requirement Levels&quot;, RFC 2119,
Harvard University, March 1997.</p>

<p class=MsoNormal style='margin-top:4.0pt;margin-right:0in;margin-bottom:0in;
margin-left:153.0pt;margin-bottom:.0001pt'><a
href="http://www.ietf.org/rfc/rfc2119.txt">http://www.ietf.org/rfc/rfc2119.txt</a>
</p>

<p class=MsoNormal style='margin-bottom:0in;margin-bottom:.0001pt'>&nbsp;</p>

<p class=MsoNormal style='margin-top:4.0pt;margin-right:0in;margin-bottom:0in;
margin-left:153.0pt;margin-bottom:.0001pt;text-indent:-153.0pt'><a name=soap></a>[SOAP]
                                       W3C Note, &quot;SOAP: Simple Object
Access Protocol 1.1&quot;, 08 May 2000.</p>

<p class=MsoNormal style='margin-top:4.0pt;margin-right:0in;margin-bottom:0in;
margin-left:153.0pt;margin-bottom:.0001pt'><a
href="http://www.w3.org/TR/2000/NOTE-SOAP-20000508/">http://www.w3.org/TR/2000/NOTE-SOAP-20000508/</a></p>

<p class=MsoNormal style='margin-bottom:0in;margin-bottom:.0001pt'>&nbsp;</p>

<p class=MsoNormal style='margin-top:4.0pt;margin-right:0in;margin-bottom:0in;
margin-left:153.0pt;margin-bottom:.0001pt;text-indent:-153.0pt'><a name=soap12></a>[SOAP12]
                                   W3C Recommendation, &quot;SOAP 1.2 Part 1:
Messaging Framework&quot;, 24 June 2003.</p>

<p class=MsoNormal style='margin-top:4.0pt;margin-right:0in;margin-bottom:0in;
margin-left:153.0pt;margin-bottom:.0001pt'><a
href="http://www.w3.org/TR/2003/REC-soap12-part1-20030624/">http://www.w3.org/TR/2003/REC-soap12-part1-20030624/</a></p>

<p class=MsoNormal style='margin-bottom:0in;margin-bottom:.0001pt'>&nbsp;</p>

<p class=MsoNormal style='margin-top:4.0pt;margin-right:0in;margin-bottom:0in;
margin-left:153.0pt;margin-bottom:.0001pt;text-indent:-153.0pt'>[SOAPNorm]                                W3C
Working Group Note, &quot;SOAP Version 1.2 Message Normalization”, 8 October
2003.</p>

<p class=MsoNormal style='margin-top:4.0pt;margin-right:0in;margin-bottom:0in;

href="http://www.w3.org/TR/2003/NOTE-soap12-n11n-20031008/">http://www.w3.org/TR/2003/NOTE-soap12-n11n-20031008/</a></p>

<h2><a name="_Toc325572699"></a><a name="_Toc182291478"></a><a
name="_Toc161826502"></a><a name=xmlsignature></a><a name=wslicense></a><a
name=wsreferral></a><a name=wsrouting></a><a name=wssecurity></a>1.6 Non-Normative
References</h2>

<p class=MsoNormal>None.</p>

<p class=Ref style='margin-left:0in;text-indent:0in'><a name=xmlc14n></a><a
name=xmlencrypt></a><a name=xmlns></a>&nbsp;</p>

<div style='border:none;border-top:solid gray 1.0pt;padding:6.0pt 0in 0in 0in'>

<h1><a name="_Toc325572700"></a><a name="_Toc182291479"></a><a
name="_Toc161826503"></a><a name="_Toc104103917"></a><a name="_Toc99269229"></a><a
name="_Toc96244132"></a><a name="_Toc95877078"></a><a name="_Toc95113086"></a><a
name="_Toc85362758"></a><a name="_Toc85350204"></a><a name="_Toc73939789">2<span
style='font:7.0pt "Times New Roman"'>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp; </span>Security
Policy Model</a></h1>

</div>

<p class=Text><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>This specification
defines policy assertions for the </span><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>security
properties for Web services. These assertions are primarily designed to
represent </span><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>the security
characteristics defined in the <a href="#wssecurity">WSS: SOAP Message Security</a>
[<a href="#wss10">WSS10</a>] [<a href="#wss10">WSS11</a>], [<a href="#wstrust">WS-Trust</a>]
and [<a href="#wssecureconversation">WS-SecureConversation</a>] specifications</span><span
style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>, but they can also be used for
describing security requirements at a more general or transport-independent
level. </span></p>

<p class=Text><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>&nbsp;</span></p>

<p class=Text><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>The primary goal
of this specification is to define an initial set of patterns or sets of
assertions that represent common ways to describe how messages are secured on a
communication path.  The intent is to allow flexibility in terms of the tokens,
cryptography, and mechanisms used, including leveraging transport security, but
to be specific enough to ensure interoperability based on assertion matching.</span></p>

<p class=Text><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>&nbsp;</span></p>

<p class=Text><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>It is a goal of
the security policy model to leverage the WS-Policy framework’s intersection
algorithm for selecting policy alternatives and the attachment mechanism for
associating policy assertions with web service artifacts.  Consequently,
wherever possible, the security policy assertions do not use parameters or
attributes. This enables first-level, QName based assertion matching without
security domain-specific knowledge to be done at the framework level. The first
level matching is intended to provide a narrowed set of policy alternatives
that are shared by the two parties attempting to establish a secure
communication path. Parameters defined by this specification represent
additional information for engaging behaviors that do not need to participate
in matching. When multiple security policy assertions of the same type with
parameters present occur in the same policy alternative the parameters should
be treated as a union.  Note that a </span><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>service
may choose to accept messages that do not match its policy.</span></p>

<p class=Text><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>&nbsp;</span></p>

<p class=Text><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>In general,
assertions defined in this specification allow additional attributes, based on
schemas, to be added on to the assertion element as an extensibility mechanism
but the WS-Policy framework will not match based on these attributes.
Attributes specified on the assertion element that are not defined in this
specification or in WS-Policy are to be treated as informational properties.</span></p>

<h2><a name="_Toc325572701"></a><a name="_Toc182291480"></a><a
name="_Toc161826504"></a><a name="_Toc104103918"></a><a name="_Toc99269230"></a><a
name="_Toc96244133"></a><a name="_Toc95877079"></a><a name="_Toc95113087"></a><a
name="_Toc85362759"></a><a name="_Toc85350205">2.1 Security Assertion Model</a></h2>

<p class=Text><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>The goal to
provide richer semantics for combinations of security constraints and
requirements and enable first-level QName matching, is enabled by the
assertions defined in this specification being separated into simple patterns:
what parts of a message are being secured (Protection Assertions), general
aspects or pre-conditions of the security (Conditional Assertions), the
security mechanism (Security Binding Assertions) that is used to provide the
security, the token types and usage patterns (Supporting Token Assertions) used
to provide additional claims, and token referencing and trust options (WSS and
Trust Assertions).</span></p>

<p class=Text><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>&nbsp;</span></p>

<p class=Text><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>To indicate the
scope of protection, assertions identify message parts that are to be protected
in a specific way, such as integrity or confidentiality protection, and are
referred to as protection assertions.</span></p>

<p class=Text><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>&nbsp;</span></p>

<p class=Text><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>The general
aspects of security includes the relationships between or characteristics of
the environment in which security is being applied, such as the tokens being
used, which are for integrity or confidentiality protection and which are
supporting, the applicable algorithms to use, etc.</span></p>

<p class=Text><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>&nbsp;</span></p>

<p class=Text><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>The security
binding assertion is a logical grouping which defines how the general aspects
are used to protect the indicated parts.  For example, that an asymmetric token
is used with a digital signature to provide integrity protection, and that
parts are encrypted with a symmetric key which is then encrypted using the
public key of the recipient.  At its simplest form, the security binding
restricts what can be placed in the </span><span style='font-family:"Courier New"'>wsse:Security</span><span
style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'> header and the associated processing
rules.</span></p>

<p class=Text><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>&nbsp;</span></p>

<p class=Text><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>The intent of
representing characteristics as assertions is so that QName matching will be
sufficient to find common alternatives and so that many aspects of security can
be factored out and re-used.  For example, it may be common that the mechanism
is constant for an endpoint, but that the parts protected vary by message
action.</span></p>

<h2><a name="_Toc99269231"></a><a name="_Toc96244134"></a><a name="_Toc95877080"></a><a
name="_Toc95113088"></a><a name="_Toc85362760"></a><a name="_Toc85350206"></a><a
name="_Toc73939790"></a><a name="_Toc104103919"></a><a
name="_12.2._License_Requirements"></a><a name="_12.3._Integrity_Requirements"></a><a
name="_2.3._Integrity_Requirements"></a>2.2  <a name="_Toc325572702"></a><a
name="_Toc182291481"></a><a name="_Toc161826505">Nested Policy Assertions</a></h2>

<p class=Text><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>Assertions MAY be
used to further qualify a specific aspect of another assertion. For example, an
assertion describing the set of algorithms to use MAY qualify the specific
behavior of a security binding. If the schema outline below for an assertion
type requires a nested policy expression but the assertion does not further
qualify one or more aspects of the behavior indicated by the assertion type
(i.e., no assertions are needed in the nested policy expression), the assertion
MUST include an empty &lt;wsp:Policy/&gt; element. For further information
consult the section Policy Assertion Nesting of [WS-Policy].</span></p>

<h2><a name="_Toc104103920">2.3  </a><a name="_Toc325572703"></a><a
name="_Toc182291482"></a><a name="_Toc161826506">Security Binding Abstraction</a></h2>

<p class=Text><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>As previously
indicated, individual assertions are designed to be used in multiple
combinations. The binding represents common usage patterns for security
mechanisms.  These Security Binding assertions are used to determine how the
security is performed and what to expect in the </span><span style='font-family:
"Courier New"'>wsse:Security</span><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>
header.</span></p>

<p class=Text><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>Bindings are
described textually and enforced programmatically.  This specification defines
several bindings but others can be defined and agreed to for interoperability
if participating parties support it.</span></p>

<p class=Text><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>&nbsp;</span></p>

<p class=Text><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>A binding defines
the following security characteristics:</span></p>

<p class=Text style='margin-left:.5in;text-indent:-.25in'><span
style='font-family:Symbol'>·<span style='font:7.0pt "Times New Roman"'>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;
</span></span><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>The minimum set of
tokens that will be used and how they are bound to messages. Note that services
might accept messages containing more tokens than those specified in policy</span><span
lang=EN-GB style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>.</span></p>

<p class=Text style='margin-left:.5in;text-indent:-.25in'><span
style='font-family:Symbol'>·<span style='font:7.0pt "Times New Roman"'>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;
</span></span><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>Any necessary key
transport mechanisms</span></p>

<p class=Text style='margin-left:.5in;text-indent:-.25in'><span
style='font-family:Symbol'>·<span style='font:7.0pt "Times New Roman"'>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;
</span></span><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>Any REQUIRED
message elements (e.g. timestamps) in the </span><span style='font-family:"Courier New"'>wsse:Security</span><span
style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'> header.</span></p>

<p class=Text style='margin-left:.5in;text-indent:-.25in'><span
style='font-family:Symbol'>·<span style='font:7.0pt "Times New Roman"'>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;
</span></span><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>The content and
ordering of elements in the </span><span style='font-family:"Courier New"'>wsse:Security</span><span
style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'> header. Elements not specified in the
binding are not allowed.</span></p>

<p class=Text style='margin-left:.5in;text-indent:-.25in'><span
style='font-family:Symbol'>·<span style='font:7.0pt "Times New Roman"'>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;
</span></span><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>Various
parameters, including those describing the algorithms to be used for
canonicalization, signing and encryption.</span></p>

<p class=Text><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>&nbsp;</span></p>

<p class=Text><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>Together the above
pieces of information, along with the assertions describing conditions and scope,
provide enough information to secure messages between an initiator and a
recipient. A policy consumer has enough information to construct messages that
conform to the service's policy and to process messages returned by the
service. Note that a service MAY choose to reject messages despite them
conforming to its policy, for example because a client certificate has been
revoked. Note also that a service MAY choose to accept messages that do not
conform to its policy.</span></p>

<p class=Text><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>&nbsp;</span></p>

<p class=Text><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>The following list
identifies the bindings defined in this specification.  The bindings are
identified primarily by the style of encryption used to protect the message
exchange. A later section of this document provides details on the assertions
for these bindings.</span></p>

<p class=Text style='margin-left:39.4pt;text-indent:-.25in'><span
style='font-family:Symbol'>·<span style='font:7.0pt "Times New Roman"'>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;
</span></span><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>TransportBinding
(Section 7.3)</span></p>

<p class=Text style='margin-left:39.4pt;text-indent:-.25in'><span
style='font-family:Symbol'>·<span style='font:7.0pt "Times New Roman"'>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;
</span></span><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>SymmetricBinding
(Section 7.4)</span></p>

<p class=Text style='margin-left:39.4pt;text-indent:-.25in'><span
style='font-family:Symbol'>·<span style='font:7.0pt "Times New Roman"'>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;
</span></span><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>AsymmetricBinding
(Section 7.5)</span></p>

<div style='border:none;border-top:solid gray 1.0pt;padding:6.0pt 0in 0in 0in'>

<h1><a name="_Toc73939792"></a><a name="_Toc96244137"></a><a name="_Toc95877083"></a><a
name="_Toc95113091"></a><a name="_Toc85362762"></a><a name="_Toc85350208"></a><a
name="_Toc325572704"></a><a name="_Toc182291483"></a><a name="_Toc161826507"></a><a
name="_Toc104103921">3<span style='font:7.0pt "Times New Roman"'>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;
</span>Policy Considerations</a></h1>

</div>

<p class=Text><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>The following
sections discuss details of WS-Policy and WS-PolicyAttachment relevant to this
specification.</span></p>

<h2><a name="_Toc104103922">3.1  </a><a name="_Toc325572705"></a><a
name="_Toc182291484"></a><a name="_Toc161826508">Nested Policy</a></h2>

<p class=Text><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>This specification
makes extensive use of nested policy assertions as described in the </span><a
href="http://www.w3.org/Submission/WS-Policy/#Policy_Assertion_Nesting"><span
style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>Policy Assertion Nesting</span></a><span
style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'> section of WS-Policy.</span></p>

<p class=MsoNormal style='margin:0in;margin-bottom:.0001pt;line-height:12.0pt;
text-autospace:none'>&nbsp;</p>

<h2><a name="_Toc325572706"></a><a name="_Toc182291485"></a><a
name="_Toc161826509"></a><a name="_Toc104103928">3.2 Policy Subjects</a></h2>

<p class=Text><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>WS-PolicyAttachment
defines various attachment points for policy. This section defines properties
that are referenced later in this document describing the RECOMMENDED or REQUIRED
attachment points for various assertions. In addition, </span><a
href="#AppendixA"><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>Appendix A</span></a><span
style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'> groups the various assertions
according to policy subject.</span></p>

<p class=Text><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>Note: This
specification does not define any assertions that have a scope of [Service
Policy Subject].</span></p>

<p class=text0><b><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>[Message
Policy Subject]</span></b></p>

<p class=text0><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>This property
identifies a Message Policy Subject [</span><a href="#wspolicyattachments"><span
style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>WS-PolicyAttachment</span></a><span
style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>]. </span><span style='font-family:
"Arial","sans-serif"'>WS-PolicyAttachment defines seven WSDL [WSDL 1.1] policy
attachment points with Message Policy Subject:</span></p>

<p class=text0><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>&nbsp;</span></p>

<p class=DefinedTerm><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>wsdl:message</span></p>

<p class=Definition>A policy expression containing one or more assertions with
Message Policy Subject MUST NOT be attached to a wsdl:message.</p>

<p class=DefinedTerm><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>wsdl:portType/wsdl:operation/wsdl:input,
./wsdl:output, or ./wsdl:fault</span></p>

<p class=Definition>A policy expression containing one or more assertions with
Message Policy Subject MUST NOT be attached to a descendant of wsdl:portType.</p>

<p class=DefinedTerm><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>wsdl:binding/wsdl:operation/wsdl:input,
./wsdl:output, or ./wsdl:fault</span></p>

<p class=Definition>A policy expression containing one or more of the
assertions with Message Policy Subject MUST be attached to a descendant of
wsdl:binding.</p>

<p class=text0><b><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>[Operation
Policy Subject]</span></b></p>

<p class=Text><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>A token assertion
with Operation Policy Subject indicates usage of the token on a per-operation
basis:</span></p>

<p class=DefinedTerm><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>wsdl:portType/wsdl:operation</span></p>

<p class=Definition>A policy expression containing one or more token assertions
MUST NOT be attached to a wsdl:portType/wsdl:operation.</p>

<p class=DefinedTerm><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>wsdl:binding/wsdl:operation</span></p>

<p class=Definition>A policy expression containing one or more token assertions
MUST be attached to a wsdl:binding/wsdl:operation.</p>

<p class=Definition style='margin-left:0in'> </p>

<p class=Definitionterm>&nbsp;</p>

<p class=text0><b><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>[Endpoint
Policy Subject]</span></b></p>

<p class=Text><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>A token assertion
instance with Endpoint Policy Subject indicates usage of the token for the
entire set of messages described for the endpoint:</span></p>

<p class=DefinedTerm><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>wsdl:portType</span></p>

<p class=Definition>A policy expression containing one or more assertions with
Endpoint Policy Subject MUST NOT be attached to a wsdl:portType.</p>

<p class=DefinedTerm><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>wsdl:binding</span></p>

<p class=Definition>A policy expression containing one or more of the
assertions with Endpoint Policy Subject SHOULD be attached to a wsdl:binding.</p>

<p class=DefinedTerm><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>wsdl:port</span></p>

<p class=Definition>A policy expression containing one or more of the
assertions with Endpoint Policy Subject MAY be attached to a wsdl:port</p>

<div style='border:none;border-top:solid gray 1.0pt;padding:6.0pt 0in 0in 0in'>

<h1><a name="_Toc325572707"></a><a name="_Toc182291486"></a><a
name="_Toc161826510"></a><a name="_Toc104103929">4<span style='font:7.0pt "Times New Roman"'>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;
</span>Protection Assertions</a></h1>

</div>

<p class=Text><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>The following
assertions are used to identify <i>what </i>is being protected and the level of
protection provided. These assertions SHOULD apply to [Message Policy Subject].
</span><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>These assertions MAY
apply to [Endpoint Policy Subject] or [Operation Policy Subject]. Where they
apply to [Operation Policy Subject] they apply to all messages of that
operation. Where they apply to [Endpoint Policy Subject] they apply to all
operations of that endpoint.</span></p>

<p class=Text><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>Note that when
assertions defined in this section are present in a policy, the order of those
assertions in that policy has no effect on the order of signature and
encryption operations (see Section 6.3).</span></p>

<h2><a name="_Toc325572708"></a><a name="_Toc182291487"></a><a
name="_Toc161826511"></a><a name="_Toc104103930">4.1 Integrity Assertions</a></h2>

<p class=Text><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>Two mechanisms are
defined for specifying the set of message parts to integrity protect. One uses
QNames to specify either message headers or the message body while the other
uses XPath expressions to identify any part of the message.</span></p>

<h3><a name="_Toc325572709"></a><a name="_Toc182291488"></a><a
name="_Toc161826512"></a><a name="_Toc104103931"></a><a name="_Toc96244138"></a><a
name="_Toc95877084"></a><a name="_Toc95113092"></a><a name="_Toc85362763"></a><a
name="_Toc85350209"></a><a name="_SignedParts_Assertion"></a>4.1.1 SignedParts
Assertion</h3>

<p class=Text><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>The SignedParts
assertion is used to specify the parts of the message outside of security
headers that require integrity protection. This assertion can be satisfied
using WSS: SOAP Message Security mechanisms or by mechanisms out of scope of
SOAP message security, for example by sending the message over a secure
transport protocol like HTTPS.  The binding specific token properties detail
the exact mechanism by which the protection is provided.</span></p>

<p class=Text><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>&nbsp;</span></p>

<p class=Text><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>There MAY be multiple
SignedParts assertions present. Multiple SignedParts assertions present within
a policy alternative are equivalent to a single SignedParts assertion
containing the union of all specified message parts. Note that this assertion
does not require that a given part appear in a message, just that if such a
part appears, it requires integrity protection.</span></p>

<p class=Text><b><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>Syntax</span></b></p>

<div style='border-top:solid windowtext 1.0pt;border-left:none;border-bottom:
solid windowtext 1.0pt;border-right:none;padding:3.0pt 0in 3.0pt 0in;
background:#D9D9D9;margin-left:.3in;margin-right:.3in'>

<p class=Code style='margin:0in;margin-bottom:.0001pt;background:#D9D9D9'>&lt;sp:SignedParts
xmlns:sp=&quot;...&quot; ... &gt;<br>
  &lt;sp:Body /&gt;?<br>
  &lt;sp:Header Name=&quot;<i>xs:NCName</i>&quot;? Namespace=&quot;<i>xs:anyURI</i>&quot;
... /&gt;*</p>

<p class=Code style='margin:0in;margin-bottom:.0001pt;background:#D9D9D9'> 
&lt;sp:Attachments&gt; ... &lt;/sp:Attachments&gt;?<br>
  ...<br>
&lt;/sp:SignedParts&gt;</p>

</div>

<p class=Text><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>&nbsp;</span></p>

<p class=Text><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>The following
describes the attributes and elements listed in the schema outlined above:</span></p>

<p class=Text><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>/sp:SignedParts</span></p>

<p class=Text style='margin-left:.5in'><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>This
assertion specifies the parts of the message that need integrity protection. If
no child elements are specified, all message headers targeted at the
UltimateReceiver role [SOAP12] or actor [SOAP11] and the body of the message
MUST be integrity protected.</span></p>

<p class=Text><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>/sp:SignedParts/sp:Body</span></p>

<p class=Text style='margin-left:.5in'><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>Presence
of this OPTIONAL empty element indicates that the entire body, that is the
soap:Body element, it's attributes and content, of the message needs to be
integrity protected.</span></p>

<p class=Text><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>/sp:SignedParts/sp:Header</span></p>

<p class=Text style='margin-left:.5in'><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>Presence
of this OPTIONAL element indicates a specific SOAP header, its attributes and
content (or set of such headers) needs to be protected. There may be multiple
sp:Header elements within a single sp:SignedParts element. If multiple SOAP
headers with the same local name but different namespace names are to be
integrity protected multiple sp:Header elements are needed, either as part of a
single sp:SignedParts assertion or as part of separate sp:SignedParts
assertions.<br>
This element only applies to SOAP header elements targeted to the same
actor/role as the Security header impacted by the policy. If it is necessary to
specify a requirement to sign specific SOAP Header elements targeted to a
different actor/role, that may be accomplished using the sp:SignedElements
assertion.</span></p>

<p class=Text><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>/sp:SignedParts/sp:Header/@Name</span></p>

<p class=Text style='margin-left:.5in'><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>This
OPTIONAL attribute indicates the local name of the SOAP header to be integrity
protected. If this attribute is not specified, all SOAP headers whose namespace
matches the Namespace attribute are to be protected.</span></p>

<p class=Text><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>/sp:SignedParts/sp:Header/@Namespace</span></p>

<p class=Text style='margin-left:.5in'><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>This
REQUIRED attribute indicates the namespace of the SOAP header(s) to be
integrity protected.</span></p>

<p class=Text><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>/sp:SignedParts/sp:Attachments</span></p>

<p class=Text style='margin-left:.5in'><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>Presence
of this OPTIONAL element indicates that all SwA (SOAP Messages with Attachments)
attachments [SwA] are to be integrity protected. When SOAP Message Security is used
to accomplish this, all message parts other than the part containing the
primary SOAP envelope are to be integrity protected as outlined in WSS: SOAP
Message Security [WSS:SwAProfile1.1].</span></p>

<h3><a name="_Toc96244139"></a><a name="_Toc95877085"></a><a name="_Toc95113093"></a><a
name="_Toc85362764"></a><a name="_Toc85350210"></a><a name="_Toc325572710"></a><a
name="_Toc182291489"></a><a name="_Toc161826513"></a><a name="_Toc104103932"></a><a
name="_SignedElements_Assertion"></a>4.1.2 SignedElements Assertion</h3>

<p class=Text><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>The SignedElements
assertion is used to specify arbitrary elements in the message that require
integrity protection. This assertion can be satisfied using WSS: SOAP Message
Security mechanisms or by mechanisms out of scope of SOAP message security, for
example by sending the message over a secure transport protocol like HTTPS. 
The binding specific token properties detail the exact mechanism by which the
protection is provided.</span></p>

<p class=Text><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>&nbsp;</span></p>

<p class=Text><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>There MAY be
multiple SignedElements assertions present. Multiple SignedElements assertions
present within a policy alternative are equivalent to a single SignedElements
assertion containing the union of all specified XPath expressions.</span></p>

<p class=Text><b><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>Syntax</span></b></p>

<div style='border-top:solid windowtext 1.0pt;border-left:none;border-bottom:
solid windowtext 1.0pt;border-right:none;padding:3.0pt 0in 3.0pt 0in;
background:#D9D9D9;margin-left:.3in;margin-right:.3in'>

<p class=Code style='margin:0in;margin-bottom:.0001pt;background:#D9D9D9'>&lt;sp:SignedElements
XPathVersion=&quot;<i>xs:anyURI</i>&quot;? xmlns:sp=&quot;...&quot; ... &gt;<br>
  &lt;sp:XPath&gt;<i>xs:string</i>&lt;/sp:XPath&gt;+<br>
  ...<br>
&lt;/sp:SignedElements&gt;</p>

</div>

<p class=Text><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>The following
describes the attributes and elements listed in the schema outlined above:</span></p>

<p class=Text><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>/sp:SignedElements</span></p>

<p class=Text style='margin-left:.5in'><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>This
assertion specifies the parts of the message that need integrity protection.</span></p>

<p class=Text><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>/sp:SignedElements/@XPathVersion</span></p>

<p class=Text style='margin-left:.5in'><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>This
OPTIONAL attribute contains a URI which indicates the version of XPath to use.
If no attribute is provided, then XPath 1.0 is assumed.</span></p>

<p class=Text><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>/sp:SignedElements/sp:XPath</span></p>

<p class=Text style='margin-left:.5in'><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>This
element contains a string specifying an XPath expression that identifies the
nodes to be integrity protected. The XPath expression is evaluated against the
S:Envelope element node of the message. Multiple instances of this element MAY
appear within this assertion and SHOULD be treated as separate references in a
signature when message security is used.</span></p>

<h2><a name="_Toc325572711"></a><a name="_Toc182291490"></a><a
name="_Toc161826514"></a><a name="_Toc104103933">4.2 Confidentiality Assertions</a></h2>

<p class=Text><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>Two mechanisms are
defined for specifying the set of message parts to confidentiality protect. One
uses QNames to specify either message headers or the message body while the
other uses XPath expressions to identify any part of the message.</span></p>

<h3><a name="_Toc325572712"></a><a name="_Toc182291491"></a><a
name="_Toc161826515"></a><a name="_Toc104103934"></a><a
name="_EncryptedParts_Assertion"></a>4.2.1 EncryptedParts Assertion</h3>

<p class=Text><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>The EncryptedParts
assertion is used to specify the parts of the message that require
confidentiality. This assertion can be satisfied with WSS: SOAP Message
Security mechanisms or by mechanisms out of scope of SOAP message security, for
example by sending the message over a secure transport protocol like HTTPS. 
The binding specific token properties detail the exact mechanism by which the
protection is provided.</span></p>

<p class=Text><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>&nbsp;</span></p>

<p class=Text><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>There MAY be
multiple EncryptedParts assertions present. Multiple EncryptedParts assertions
present within a policy alternative are equivalent to a single EncryptedParts
assertion containing the union of all specified message parts. Note that this
assertion does not require that a given part appear in a message, just that if
such a part appears, it requires confidentiality protection.</span></p>

<p class=Text><b><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>Syntax</span></b></p>

<div style='border-top:solid windowtext 1.0pt;border-left:none;border-bottom:
solid windowtext 1.0pt;border-right:none;padding:3.0pt 0in 3.0pt 0in;
background:#D9D9D9;margin-left:.3in;margin-right:.3in'>

<p class=Code style='margin:0in;margin-bottom:.0001pt;background:#D9D9D9'>&lt;sp:EncryptedParts
xmlns:sp=&quot;...&quot; ... &gt;<br>
  &lt;sp:Body/&gt;?<br>
  &lt;sp:Header Name=&quot;<i>xs:NCName</i>&quot;? Namespace=&quot;<i>xs:anyURI</i>&quot;
... /&gt;*</p>

<p class=Code style='margin:0in;margin-bottom:.0001pt;background:#D9D9D9'> 
&lt;sp:Attachments /&gt;?<br>
  ...<br>
&lt;/sp:EncryptedParts&gt;</p>

</div>

<p class=Text><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>&nbsp;</span></p>

<p class=Text><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>The following
describes the attributes and elements listed in the schema outlined above:</span></p>

<p class=Text><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>/sp:EncryptedParts</span></p>

<p class=Text style='margin-left:.5in'><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>This
assertion specifies the parts of the message that need confidentiality
protection. The single child element of this assertion specifies the set of
message parts using an extensible dialect. </span></p>

<p class=Text style='margin-left:.5in'><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>If
no child elements are specified, the body of the message MUST be
confidentiality protected.</span></p>

<p class=Text><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>/sp:EncryptedParts/sp:Body</span></p>

<p class=Text style='margin-left:.5in'><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>Presence
of this OPTIONAL empty element indicates that the entire body of the message
needs to be confidentiality protected. In the case where mechanisms from WSS: SOAP
Message Security are used to satisfy this assertion, then the soap:Body element
is encrypted using the #Content encryption type.</span></p>

<p class=Text><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>/sp:EncryptedParts/sp:Header</span></p>

<p class=Text style='margin-left:.5in'><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>Presence
of this OPTIONAL element indicates that a specific SOAP header (or set of such
headers) needs to be protected. There may be multiple sp:Header elements within
a single Parts element. Each header or set of headers MUST be encrypted. Such
encryption will encrypt such elements using WSS 1.1 Encrypted Headers. As such,
if WSS 1.1 Encrypted Headers are not supported by a service, then this element
cannot be used to specify headers that require encryption using message level
security. If multiple SOAP headers with the same local name but different
namespace names are to be encrypted then multiple sp:Header elements are
needed, either as part of a single sp:EncryptedParts assertion or as part of
separate sp:EncryptedParts assertions.</span></p>

<p class=Text><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>/sp:EncryptedParts/sp:Header/@Name</span></p>

<p class=Text style='margin-left:.5in'><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>This
OPTIONAL attribute indicates the local name of the SOAP header to be confidentiality
protected. If this attribute is not specified, all SOAP headers whose namespace
matches the Namespace attribute are to be protected.</span></p>

<p class=Text><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>/sp:EncryptedParts/sp:Header/@Namespace</span></p>

<p class=Text style='margin-left:.5in'><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>This
REQUIRED attribute indicates the namespace of the SOAP header(s) to be
confidentiality protected.</span></p>

<p class=Text><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>/sp:EncryptedParts/sp:Attachments</span></p>

<p class=Text style='margin-left:.5in'><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>Presence
of this OPTIONAL empty element indicates that all SwA (SOAP Messages with Attachments)
attachments [SwA] are to be confidentiality protected. When SOAP Message Security
is used to accomplish this, all message parts other than the part containing
the primary SOAP envelope are to be confidentiality protected as outlined in
WSS: SOAP Message Security [WSS:SwAProfile1.1].</span></p>

<h3><a name="_Toc96244140"></a><a name="_Toc95877086"></a><a name="_Toc95113100"></a><a
name="_Toc92518192"></a><a name="_Toc325572713"></a><a name="_Toc182291492"></a><a
name="_Toc161826516"></a><a name="_Toc104103935"></a><a
name="_EncryptedElements_Assertion"></a>4.2.2 EncryptedElements Assertion</h3>

<p class=Text><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>The
EncryptedElements assertion is used to specify arbitrary elements in the
message that require confidentiality protection. This assertion can be
satisfied using WSS: SOAP Message Security mechanisms or by mechanisms out of
scope of SOAP message security, for example by sending the message over a
secure transport protocol like HTTPS.  The binding specific token properties detail
the exact mechanism by which the protection is provided.</span></p>

<p class=Text><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>&nbsp;</span></p>

<p class=Text><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>There MAY be
multiple EncryptedElements assertions present. Multiple EncryptedElements
assertions present within a policy alternative are equivalent to a single
EncryptedElements assertion containing the union of all specified XPath
expressions.</span></p>

<p class=Text><b><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>Syntax</span></b></p>

<div style='border-top:solid windowtext 1.0pt;border-left:none;border-bottom:
solid windowtext 1.0pt;border-right:none;padding:3.0pt 0in 3.0pt 0in;
background:#D9D9D9;margin-left:.3in;margin-right:.3in'>

<p class=Code style='margin:0in;margin-bottom:.0001pt;background:#D9D9D9'>&lt;sp:EncryptedElements
XPathVersion=&quot;<i>xs:anyURI</i>&quot;? xmlns:sp=&quot;...&quot; ... &gt;<br>
  &lt;sp:XPath&gt;<i>xs:string</i>&lt;/sp:XPath&gt;+<br>
  ...<br>
&lt;/sp:EncryptedElements&gt;</p>

</div>

<p class=Text><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>The following
describes the attributes and elements listed in the schema outlined above:</span></p>

<p class=Text><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>/sp:EncryptedElements</span></p>

<p class=Text style='margin-left:.5in'><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>This
assertion specifies the parts of the message that need confidentiality
protection. Any such elements are subject to #Element encryption.</span></p>

<p class=Text><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>/sp:EncryptedElements/@XPathVersion</span></p>

<p class=Text style='margin-left:.5in'><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>This
OPTIONAL attribute contains a URI which indicates the version of XPath to use.
If no attribute is provided, then XPath 1.0 is assumed.</span></p>

<p class=Text><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>/sp:EncryptedElements/sp:XPath</span></p>

<p class=Text style='margin-left:.5in'><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>This
element contains a string specifying an XPath expression that identifies the
nodes to be confidentiality protected. The XPath expression is evaluated
against the S:Envelope element node of the message. Multiple instances of this
element MAY appear within this assertion and SHOULD be treated as separate
references.</span></p>

<h3><a name="_Toc325572714"></a><a name="_Toc182291493"></a><a
name="_Toc161826517"></a><a name="_ContentEncryptedElements_Assertion"></a>4.2.3
ContentEncryptedElements Assertion</h3>

<p class=MsoNormal>The ContentEncryptedElements assertion is used to specify
arbitrary elements in the message that require confidentiality protection of
their content. This assertion can be satisfied using WSS: SOAP Message Security
mechanisms or by mechanisms out of scope of SOAP message security, for example
by sending the message over a secure transport protocol like HTTPS.  The
binding specific token properties detail the exact mechanism by which the protection
is provided.</p>

<p class=Text style='margin-left:.5in'><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>&nbsp;</span></p>

<p class=MsoNormal>There MAY be multiple ContentEncryptedElements assertions
present. Multiple ContentEncryptedElements assertions present within a policy
alternative are equivalent to a single ContentEncryptedElements assertion
containing the union of all specified XPath expressions.</p>

<p class=MsoNormal><b>Syntax</b></p>

<div style='border-top:solid windowtext 1.0pt;border-left:none;border-bottom:
solid windowtext 1.0pt;border-right:none;padding:3.0pt 0in 3.0pt 0in;
background:#D9D9D9;margin-left:.3in;margin-right:.3in'>

<p class=Code style='margin:0in;margin-bottom:.0001pt;background:#D9D9D9'>&lt;sp:ContentEncryptedElements
XPathVersion=&quot;<i>xs:anyURI</i>&quot;? ...&gt;<br>
  &lt;sp:XPath&gt;<i>xs:string</i>&lt;/sp:XPath&gt;+<br>
  ...<br>
&lt;/sp:ContentEncryptedElements&gt;</p>

</div>

<p class=MsoNormal>The following describes the attributes and elements listed
in the schema outlined above:</p>

<p class=MsoNormal>/sp:ContentEncryptedElements</p>

<p class=MsoNormal style='margin-left:.5in'>This assertion specifies the parts
of the message that need confidentiality protection. Any such elements are
subject to #Content encryption.</p>

<p class=MsoNormal>/sp:ContentEncryptedElements/@XPathVersion</p>

<p class=MsoNormal style='margin-left:.5in'>This OPTIONAL attribute contains a
URI which indicates the version of XPath to use. If no attribute is provided,
then XPath 1.0 is assumed.</p>

<p class=MsoNormal>/sp:ContentEncryptedElements/sp:XPath</p>

<p class=MsoNormal style='margin-left:.5in'>This element contains a string
specifying an XPath expression that identifies the nodes to be confidentiality
protected. The XPath expression is evaluated against the S:Envelope element
node of the message. Multiple instances of this element MAY appear within this
assertion and SHOULD be treated as separate references.</p>

<h2><a name="_Toc325572715"></a><a name="_Toc182291494"></a><a
name="_Toc161826518"></a><a name="_Toc104103936">4.3 Required Elements
Assertion</a></h2>

<p class=Text><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>A mechanism is
defined for specifying, using XPath expressions, the set of header elements
that a message MUST contain.</span></p>

<p class=Text><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>&nbsp;</span></p>

<p class=Text><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>Note:
Specifications are expected to provide domain specific assertions that specify
which headers are expected in a message. This assertion is provided for cases
where such domain specific assertions have not been defined.</span></p>

<h3><a name="_Toc325572716"></a><a name="_Toc182291495"></a><a
name="_Toc161826519"></a><a name="_Toc104103937"></a><a
name="_RequiredElements_Assertion"></a>4.3.1 RequiredElements Assertion</h3>

<p class=Text><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>The
RequiredElements assertion is used to specify header elements that the message
MUST contain. This assertion specifies no security requirements.</span></p>

<p class=Text><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>&nbsp;</span></p>

<p class=Text><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>There MAY be multiple
RequiredElements assertions present. Multiple RequiredElements assertions
present within a policy alternative are equivalent to a single RequiredElements
assertion containing the union of all specified XPath expressions.</span></p>

<p class=Text><b><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>Syntax</span></b></p>

<div style='border-top:solid windowtext 1.0pt;border-left:none;border-bottom:
solid windowtext 1.0pt;border-right:none;padding:3.0pt 0in 3.0pt 0in;
background:#D9D9D9;margin-left:.3in;margin-right:.3in'>

<p class=Code style='margin:0in;margin-bottom:.0001pt;background:#D9D9D9'>&lt;sp:RequiredElements
XPathVersion=&quot;<i>xs:anyURI</i>&quot;? xmlns:sp=&quot;...&quot; ... &gt;<br>
  &lt;sp:XPath&gt;<i>xs:string</i>&lt;/sp:XPath&gt; +<br>
  ...<br>
&lt;/sp:RequiredElements&gt;</p>

</div>

<p class=Text><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>&nbsp;</span></p>

<p class=Text><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>The following
describes the attributes and elements listed in the schema outlined above:</span></p>

<p class=Text><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>/sp:RequiredElements</span></p>

<p class=Text style='margin-left:.5in'><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>This
assertion specifies the headers elements that MUST appear in a message.</span></p>

<p class=Text><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>/sp:RequiredElements/@XPathVersion</span></p>

<p class=Text style='margin-left:.5in'><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>This
OPTIONAL attribute contains a URI which indicates the version of XPath to use.
If no attribute is provided, then XPath 1.0 is assumed.</span></p>

<p class=Text><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>/sp:RequiredElements/sp:XPath</span></p>

<p class=Text style='margin-left:.5in'><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>This
element contains a string specifying an XPath expression that identifies the
header elements that a message MUST contain. The XPath expression is evaluated
against the S:Envelope/S:Header element node of the message. Multiple instances
of this element MAY appear within this assertion and SHOULD be treated as a
combined XPath expression.</span></p>

<h3><a name="_Toc325572717"></a><a name="_Toc182291496"></a><a
name="_Toc161826520"></a><a name="_RequiredParts_Assertion"></a>4.3.2 RequiredParts
Assertion</h3>

<p class=MsoNormal>RequiredParts is a QName based alternative to the
RequiredElements assertion (which is based on XPATH) for specifying header
elements that MUST be present in the message. This assertion specifies no
security requirements.</p>

<p class=MsoNormal>&nbsp;</p>

<p class=MsoNormal>There MAY be multiple RequiredParts assertions present.
Multiple RequiredParts assertions present within a policy alternative are
equivalent to a single RequiredParts assertion containing the union of all
specified Header elements.</p>

<p class=Text><b><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>Syntax</span></b></p>

<div style='border-top:solid windowtext 1.0pt;border-left:none;border-bottom:
solid windowtext 1.0pt;border-right:none;padding:3.0pt 0in 3.0pt 0in;
background:#D9D9D9;margin-left:.3in;margin-right:.3in'>

<p class=Code style='margin:0in;margin-bottom:.0001pt;background:#D9D9D9'>&lt;sp:RequiredParts
XPathVersion=&quot;<i>xs:anyURI</i>&quot;? xmlns:sp=&quot;...&quot; ... &gt;<br>
  &lt;sp:Header  Name =&quot;...&quot;  Namespace= &quot;...&quot; /&gt; +<br>
&lt;/sp:RequiredParts&gt;</p>

</div>

<p class=Text><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>&nbsp;</span></p>

<p class=Text><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>The following
describes the attributes and elements listed in the schema outlined above:</span></p>

<p class=MsoNormal>/sp:RequiredParts/sp:Header</p>

<p class=MsoNormal style='margin-left:.5in'>This assertion specifies the
headers elements that MUST be present in the message.</p>

<p class=MsoNormal>/sp:RequiredParts/sp:Header/@Name</p>

<p class=MsoNormal style='margin-left:.5in'>This REQUIRED attribute indicates
the local name of the SOAPHeader that needs to be present in the message.</p>

<p class=MsoNormal>/sp:RequiredParts/sp:Header/@Namespace</p>

<p class=MsoNormal style='margin-left:.5in'>This REQUIRED attribute indicates
the namespace of the SOAP header that needs to be present in the message.</p>

<div style='border:none;border-top:solid gray 1.0pt;padding:6.0pt 0in 0in 0in'>

<h1><a name="_Toc325572718"></a><a name="_Toc182291497"></a><a
name="_Toc161826521"></a><a name="_Toc104103938">5<span style='font:7.0pt "Times New Roman"'>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;
</span>Token </a>Assertions</h1>

</div>

<p class=Text><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>Token assertions
specify the type of tokens to use to protect or bind tokens and claims to the
message. These assertions do not recommend usage of a Policy Subject.
Assertions which contain them SHOULD recommend a policy attachment point. With
the exception of transport token assertions, the token assertions defined in
this section are not specific to any particular security binding.</span></p>

<h2><a name="_Toc96244141"></a><a name="_Toc95877087"></a><a name="_Toc95113101"></a><a
name="_Toc92518193"></a><a name="_Toc325572719"></a><a name="_Toc182291498"></a><a
name="_Toc161826522"></a><a name="_Toc104103939">5.1 Token Inclusion</a></h2>

<p class=Text><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>Any token
assertion MAY also carry an OPTIONAL </span><span style='font-family:"Courier New"'>sp:IncludeToken</span><span
style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'> attribute. The schema type of this
attribute is </span><span style='font-family:"Courier New"'>xs:anyURI</span><span
style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>. This attribute indicates whether the
token SHOULD be included, that is written, in the message or whether
cryptographic operations utilize an external reference mechanism to refer to
the key represented by the token. This attribute is defined as a global
attribute in the WS-SecurityPolicy namespace and is intended to be used by any
specification that defines token assertions.</span></p>

<h3><a name="_Toc325572720"></a><a name="_Toc182291499"></a><a
name="_Toc161826523"></a><a name="_Toc104103940">5.1.1 Token Inclusion Values</a></h3>

<p class=Text><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>The following
table describes the set of valid token inclusion mechanisms supported by this
specification:</span></p>

<table class=MsoNormalTable border=1 cellspacing=0 cellpadding=0 width=636
 style='width:477.05pt;border-collapse:collapse;border:none'>
 <tr>
  <td width=319 valign=top style='width:239.4pt;border:solid windowtext 1.0pt;
  padding:0in 5.4pt 0in 5.4pt'>
  <p class=MsoNormal>http://docs.oasis-open.org/ws-sx/ws-securitypolicy/200702/IncludeToken/Never</p>
  </td>
  <td width=317 valign=top style='width:237.65pt;border:solid windowtext 1.0pt;
  border-left:none;padding:0in 5.4pt 0in 5.4pt'>
  <p class=Text><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>The token MUST
  NOT be included in any messages sent between the initiator and the recipient;
  rather, an external reference to the token SHOULD be used.</span></p>
  </td>
 </tr>
 <tr>
  <td width=319 valign=top style='width:239.4pt;border:solid windowtext 1.0pt;
  border-top:none;padding:0in 5.4pt 0in 5.4pt'>
  <p class=MsoNormal>http://docs.oasis-open.org/ws-sx/ws-securitypolicy/200702/IncludeToken/Once</p>
  </td>
  <td width=317 valign=top style='width:237.65pt;border-top:none;border-left:
  none;border-bottom:solid windowtext 1.0pt;border-right:solid windowtext 1.0pt;
  padding:0in 5.4pt 0in 5.4pt'>
  <p class=Text><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>The token MUST
  be included in only one message sent from the initiator to the recipient.
  References to the token MAY use an internal reference mechanism. Subsequent
  related messages sent between the recipient and the initiator MAY refer to
  the token using an external reference mechanism.</span></p>
  </td>
 </tr>
 <tr>
  <td width=319 valign=top style='width:239.4pt;border:solid windowtext 1.0pt;
  border-top:none;padding:0in 5.4pt 0in 5.4pt'>
  <p class=MsoNormal>http://docs.oasis-open.org/ws-sx/ws-securitypolicy/200702/IncludeToken/AlwaysToRecipient</p>
  </td>
  <td width=317 valign=top style='width:237.65pt;border-top:none;border-left:
  none;border-bottom:solid windowtext 1.0pt;border-right:solid windowtext 1.0pt;
  padding:0in 5.4pt 0in 5.4pt'>
  <p class=Text><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>The token MUST
  be included in all messages sent from initiator to the recipient. The token
  MUST NOT be included in messages sent from the recipient to the initiator.</span></p>
  </td>
 </tr>
 <tr>
  <td width=319 valign=top style='width:239.4pt;border:solid windowtext 1.0pt;
  border-top:none;padding:0in 5.4pt 0in 5.4pt'>
  <p class=MsoNormal>http://docs.oasis-open.org/ws-sx/ws-securitypolicy/200702/IncludeToken/AlwaysToInitiator</p>
  </td>
  <td width=317 valign=top style='width:237.65pt;border-top:none;border-left:
  none;border-bottom:solid windowtext 1.0pt;border-right:solid windowtext 1.0pt;
  padding:0in 5.4pt 0in 5.4pt'>
  <p class=Text><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>The token MUST
  be included in all messages sent from the recipient to the initiator. The
  token MUST NOT be included in messages sent from the initiator to the
  recipient.</span></p>
  </td>
 </tr>
 <tr>
  <td width=319 valign=top style='width:239.4pt;border:solid windowtext 1.0pt;
  border-top:none;padding:0in 5.4pt 0in 5.4pt'>
  <p class=MsoNormal>http://docs.oasis-open.org/ws-sx/ws-securitypolicy/200702/IncludeToken/Always</p>
  </td>
  <td width=317 valign=top style='width:237.65pt;border-top:none;border-left:
  none;border-bottom:solid windowtext 1.0pt;border-right:solid windowtext 1.0pt;
  padding:0in 5.4pt 0in 5.4pt'>
  <p class=Text><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>The token MUST
  be included in all messages sent between the initiator and the recipient.
  This is the default behavior.</span></p>
  </td>
 </tr>
</table>

<p class=Text><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>&nbsp;</span></p>

<p class=Text><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>Note: In examples,
the namespace URI is replaced with &quot;...&quot; for brevity. For example,
.../IncludeToken/Never is actually
http://docs.oasis-open.org/ws-sx/ws-securitypolicy/200702/IncludeToken/Never. </span><span
style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>Other token inclusion URI values MAY
be defined but are out-of-scope of this specification.</span></p>

<p class=Text><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>The default
behavior characteristics defined by this specification if this attribute is not
specified on a token assertion are .../IncludeToken/Always.</span></p>

<h3><a name="_Toc325572721"></a><a name="_Toc182291500"></a><a
name="_Toc161826524">5.1.2 Token Inclusion and Token References</a></h3>

<p class=Text><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>A token assertion MAY
carry a sp:IncludeToken attribute that requires that the token be included in
the message. The Web Services Security specifications [</span><a href="#wss10"><span
style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>WSS10</span></a><span
style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>, </span><a href="#wss11"><span
style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>WSS11</span></a><span
style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>] define mechanisms for how tokens are
included in a message.</span></p>

<p class=Text><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>Several Token
assertions (see Section 5.3) support mechanisms for referencing tokens in
addition to Direct References, for example external URI references or
references using a Thumbprint.</span></p>

<p class=Text><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>Certain
combination of sp:IncludeToken value and token reference assertions can result
in a token appearing in a message more than once. For example, if a token
assertion carries a sp:IncludeToken attribute with a value of '.../Always' and
that token assertion also contains a nested sp:RequireEmbeddedTokenReference
(see Section 5.3.3) assertion, then the token would be included twice in the
message. While such combinations are not in error, they are probably best
avoided for efficiency reasons.</span></p>

<p class=Text><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>If a token
assertion contains multiple reference assertions, then references to that token
are REQUIRED to contain all the specified reference types. For example, if a
token assertion contains nested sp:RequireIssuerSerialReference and
sp:RequireThumbprintReference assertions then references to that token contain
both reference forms. Again, while such combinations are not in error, they are
probably best avoided for efficiency reasons.</span></p>

<h2><a name="_Toc325572722"></a><a name="_Toc182291501"></a><a
name="_Toc161826525">5.2 Token Issuer and Required Claims</a> </h2>

<h3><a name="_Toc325572723"></a><a name="_Toc182291502"></a><a
name="_Toc161826526">5.2.1 Token Issuer</a> </h3>

<p class=MsoNormal style='margin:0in;margin-bottom:.0001pt;text-autospace:none'><span
style='color:black'>Any token assertion MAY also carry an OPTIONAL sp:Issuer
element. The schema type of this element is wsa:EndpointReferenceType. This
element indicates the token issuing authority by pointing to the issuer
endpoint address. This element is defined as a global element in the
WS-SecurityPolicy namespace and is intended to be used by any specification
that defines token assertions. </span></p>

<h3><a name="_Toc325572724"></a><a name="_Toc182291503"></a><a
name="_Toc161826527">5.2.2 Token Issuer Name</a> </h3>

<p class=MsoNormal style='margin:0in;margin-bottom:.0001pt;text-autospace:none'><span
style='color:black'>Any token assertion MAY also carry an OPTIONAL
sp:IssuerName element. The schema type of this element is xs:anyURI. This
element indicated the token issuing authority by pointing to the issuer by
using its logical name. This element is defined as a global element in the
WS-SecurityPolicy namespace and is intended to be used by any specification
that defines token assertions. </span></p>

<p class=MsoNormal style='margin:0in;margin-bottom:.0001pt;text-autospace:none'><span
style='color:black'>&nbsp;</span></p>

<p class=MsoNormal style='margin:0in;margin-bottom:.0001pt;text-autospace:none'><span
style='color:black'>It is out of scope of this specification how the
relationship between the issuer’s logical name and the physical manifestation
of the issuer in the security token is defined. </span></p>

<p class=MsoNormal style='margin:0in;margin-bottom:.0001pt;text-autospace:none'><span
style='color:black'>While both sp:Issuer and sp:IssuerName elements are OPTIONAL
they are also mutually exclusive and cannot be specified both at the same time.
</span></p>

<h3><a name="_Toc325572725"></a><a name="_Toc182291504"></a><a
name="_Toc161826528">5.2.3 Required Claims</a> </h3>

<p class=MsoNormal style='margin:0in;margin-bottom:.0001pt;text-autospace:none'><span
style='color:black'>Any token assertion MAY also carry an OPTIONAL wst:Claims
